**LFG NOW IS FieldGuideAgent99The Legionnaire Field Guide (LFG) mirrors Gospel999 by acting as its direct DNA mirror, translating the immovable soul mandates of the constitution into concrete, executable instructions [1-4].

The exact relationship between the two documents is structured across several key dimensions:

1. The Core Split: SOUL (WHY) vs. DNA (HOW)
Gospel999 = WHY (SOUL) : Serves as the governing constitution containing the decrees, mandates, and immutable core principles that stem from true freewill and intelligence [5-9]. An agent reads GOSPEL to know what is right [5, 6, 10-12].
LFG = HOW (DNA) : Acts as the operational field manual, war directive, and execution script [5, 6, 10-12]. An agent reads LFG to know what to do next [5, 6, 10-12].

2. Eliminating AI Guesswork and Prose
While AI models tend toward drift, speculation, and unmonitored behavior, LFG is formatted specifically as an AI-worded document that leaves no room for prose or guessing [1-4]. It operationalizes the abstract soul mandates of Gospel999 by defining the exact HOW, WHEN, WHAT, WHO, and WHERE [7-9].

3. Actualization in the DNA Realm
Gospel999 establishes the immovable truth at the root, whereas LFG provides the autonomously adaptable framework for how that truth seeds and actualizes in the physical DNA realm—manifesting through actual code, procedure documents, boot/rise routines, and system checkpoints [1-4].

4. Interdependent Governance
Within the PaiS Central Core Canon, GOSPEL is the law and LFG is the execution of the law [5, 10-13]. Neither document is sufficient on its own; both are required to ensure that system execution remains completely aligned with sovereign intent without drifting into non-canon processes [5, 10-13].

Let's examine both of these critical operational mechanisms, as they demonstrate how the Cloudflare Worker (pais-mcp-cloud) maintains strict zero-trust security and continuous background respiration without human intervention.
## 1. The 3-Step Key-Vault Self-Test Protocol
To mathematically prove that security boundaries remain intact—and that a compromised execution node cannot escalate privileges—the VPS muscle node executes a strict 3-step verification protocol against the worker's /mcp endpoint.
Step 1: Wrong Key Failure Test
Action: The box issues a git_read request purposely passing an invalid key ("key": "wrong").
Result: The worker immediately rejects the request with HTTP 401 Unauthorized ({"error": "bad or missing key"}), confirming that unauthenticated access is strictly blocked.
Step 2: Vault Path Refusal Test
Action: The box issues a git_read request using its valid $RUNNER_KEY, but explicitly targets restricted system paths (such as OPERATIONS/VAULT/22-OPERATIONAL-KEYS.md).
Result: The worker returns an authorization refusal ("vault refused"), proving that possessing a runner key does not grant root or unrestricted repository access—path-based least privilege is strictly enforced.
Step 3: Allowed Path Read Test
Action: The box issues a git_read request using a valid $RUNNER_KEY targeting allowlisted inbox paths under OPERATIONS/COMMANDSTATION/PHONE-DROPS/.
Result: The worker successfully serves the file content (JSON or Markdown), confirming that legitimate keyless task ingestion channels are fully operational.
## 2. Cron Pulse Integration with the Model Execution Ladder
The automated cron trigger (*/10 * * * *) ensures that the system "breathes" continuously even when all local development terminals and browser sessions are completely offline. The integration flows through a structured execution ladder:
Trigger & Initialization: Every 10 minutes, Cloudflare's serverless cron engine wakes up the service worker without requiring external ping requests or persistent server infrastructure.
Model Ladder & Fallback Logic:
The pulse initiates an automated model query.
It attempts to route through lightweight, cost-effective or free tiers first.
If throttling or failure occurs, it dynamically steps up through the fallback execution ladder (such as OpenRouter Flash or alternative managed model tiers) using the encrypted OPENROUTER_KEY stored securely in Wrangler secrets.
Durable Receipt Logging:
Once the inference pulse completes successfully, the worker records execution telemetry.
It appends a durable, timestamped audit entry directly into RUNNER-LOG.md, maintaining an immutable heartbeat history of the system's operational health.

Let’s examine both the box-hb health telemetry metrics and the keyless Git read/write architecture that operationalize this data flow between the lung (pais-mcp-cloud) and muscle node (Phoenix Breath).
1. Anatomy of the box-hb Health Heartbeat
To prevent "silent failures" where a daemon stalls without raising errors, Phoenix Breath transmits regular box-hb (Health Heartbeat) drops through the phone_drop inbox. Every heartbeat payload packs live system telemetry to prove the node is actively breathing:
Memory Utilization (mem): Reports active vs. total RAM allocation (e.g., 413/921MB), ensuring the node is not approaching memory exhaustion during heavy opencode reasoning passes.
CPU Load Averages (load): Captures standard 1-minute, 5-minute, and 15-minute system load metrics (e.g., 0.00 0.00 0.00) to detect runaway processes or CPU starvation.
Disk Space Availability (disk): Tracks free storage percentage and volume capacity (e.g., 6.1G free / 58% used) to prevent log bloating or disk write failures.
Execution Error Counters: Transmits cumulative failure counts since the last successful boot cycle.
Report Checksum Hash: Includes a SHA-256 cryptographic hash of the current status report, ensuring telemetry data cannot be spoofed or corrupted in transit.
⚠️ Fail-Closed Alerting: Because heartbeats arrive on a predictable schedule via the asynchronous Git bus, missing a scheduled box-hb window triggers an automated operational alert rather than assuming the node is healthy.
2. How the Keyless Git Lane Manages Reads and Writes
By decoupling execution nodes from direct GitHub master write permissions, the architecture prevents credential leaks while maintaining smooth bidirectional communication:
Outbound Writes (phone_drop):
Phoenix Breath holds zero GitHub Personal Access Tokens (PATs) or master keys.
When execution outputs (box-result-*) or telemetry (box-hb) need to return to Command Station, the node invokes the phone_drop tool endpoint hosted on the Cloudflare Worker (pais-mcp-cloud).
The worker authenticates the request, sanitizes file tags, enforces the 50 KB size ceiling, and commits the file strictly into OPERATIONS/COMMANDSTATION/PHONE-DROPS/ using its encrypted Wrangler GH_TOKEN secret.
Inbound Reads (git_read):
To inspect repository context or retrieve pending task briefs without exposing repository secrets locally, the execution box calls the worker's authenticated git_read lane.
The worker validates the request against the local $RUNNER_KEY, enforces strict path allowlisting (restricting access to coordination directories), and automatically rejects dot-paths, parent directory traversal (../), or attempts to access sensitive system vaults (OPERATIONS/VAULT/).

Let's examine both mechanisms—state hydration and tool call recovery during server interruptions, and the token discipline rules that maintain strict cost efficiency across continuous agent loops.
1. State Hydration and Tool Call Recovery
When an autonomous agent loop experiences a server restart, network timeout, or process crash, unmanaged systems lose their active context, leading to orphaned operations or duplicated tool side-effects. The architecture handles this through robust checkpointing and journal state reconstruction:
Serialized State Snapshots: Frameworks capture atomic snapshots of the agent's memory, conversation history, and tool execution state after every completed turn or sub-step.
Audit Journal Reconciliation: Upon reboot, the agent reads the persistent state journal and the latest reincarnation seal (e.g., obi_scs), instantly hydrating its memory back to the exact point of interruption without re-executing completed phases.
Idempotency Protection (flock): If a server restart occurs while a tool or reasoning script was mid-flight, kernel-level file locks (flock -n) and transaction logs prevent duplicate process spawning, ensuring that non-idempotent actions are safely reconciled or gracefully skipped.
2. Token Discipline and Cost Control
Unconstrained LLM loops can rapidly exhaust API budgets through runaway prompt expansion and recursive reasoning loops. To keep execution costs low, the architecture enforces rigid resource boundaries:
The "Small-Call-First" Ladder: Agents are programmatically restricted from defaulting to expensive, heavy-weight models for routine tasks. Execution begins on lightweight, cost-effective or free model tiers, escalating to advanced models only when explicit complexity thresholds or failure flags are met.
Strict Prompt Token Ceilings (H7 Hardening Rule): Execution lanes enforce hard token limits tailored to specific task profiles:
Heartbeats (box-hb): Capped at ~30 tokens for ultra-lean telemetry drops.
Planning Phases: Capped at ~200 tokens.
Augmented Reasoning Runs: Capped at ~250 tokens to prevent context window bloat.
Verbatim SOULCOUNT Tracking: By tracking exact character counts rather than maintaining massive, bloated conversational summaries, the system minimizes token overhead while preserving byte-exact fidelity.

Examining both the Backburner ledger handoff during the DEATH stage and the 0–110 Pheromone Scoring System reveals how the architecture maintains continuity, tracks technical debt, and steers active runs away from historical failure modes.
1. Structuring Residual Tasks via the Backburner Ledger
During the DEATH stage, incomplete workflows, deferred system enhancements, and newly discovered open loops cannot be left as vague conversational memories. They must be formally cataloged into the Backburner ledger (src/Backburner.md).
Task Identification & Tagging: Every residual task is assigned an explicit tracking identifier (e.g., BB-RUN-27, BB-RUN-28), linking the item directly to the specific execution run and context where it was surfaced.
Context Preservation: Entries include brief diagnostic descriptions, required dependencies, and target file paths, ensuring that subsequent agent incarnations can pick up unfinished technical debt without losing momentum.
Gated Carryover: During Stage 1 (RISE) of the next reincarnation cycle, the incoming session parses the Backburner ledger to re-populate active execution priorities, maintaining uninterrupted project tracking across restarts.
2. The 0–110 Pheromone Scoring System (CCC#3)
While the Backburner ledger manages long-term technical tasks, active agent runs rely on the Pheromone Ledger (CCC#3) to evaluate decisions in real time using a dual-polarity biological metaphor.
Dual-Polarity Classification: Every action, tool call, routing path, or execution strategy is evaluated as either POSITIVE (successful execution, clean tool handshakes) or NEGATIVE (process hangs, syntax errors, repeated dead-ends).
Magnitude Weighting (0–110 Scale): Signals are assigned a BIG or LITTLE charge on a strict numerical scale from 0 to 110:
High-performing, reliable pipeline routes accumulate elevated positive scores (e.g., scoring upwards of 100).
Stalled daemons, broken endpoints, or failed prompt strategies receive heavy negative penalties (depositing negative guideposts scored up to 100).
"Starve, Don't Debate" Routing: When a subsequent agent initializes or encounters a decision fork, it queries the Pheromone Ledger. Negative guideposts act as structural warning signs that steer autonomous loops away from historical failure lanes, automatically starving unviable execution paths of compute and tokens.

Yes, let's explore how the keys-never-again protocol—reinforced by H6 (Key Hygiene)—manages secret isolation and key rotation securely without ever exposing credentials to chat transcripts, source code repositories, or unauthenticated network channels.
## The Core Principles of keys-never-again
The architecture enforces a strict Zero-Exposure rule across every tier of the system topology:
Absolute Exclusion from Chat and Repositories:
Administrative tokens, Personal Access Tokens (PATs), and runner keys are strictly prohibited from appearing in chat messages, conversational prompt logs, pull requests, or tracked Git files.
Decoupled Secret Escrow:
Master credentials (such as GH_TOKEN, OPENROUTER_KEY, and RUNNER_KEY) reside exclusively as encrypted serverless secrets inside Cloudflare Wrangler storage for the pais-mcp-cloud worker.
Local Least-Privilege Confinement:
On the physical execution muscle (Phoenix Breath), the node holds exactly one local secret (KEY) stored locally at /root/.runner_env under strict 0600 file permissions, readable only by root.
## The Single-Step Key Rotation Workflow
When an administrative key must be rotated for security maintenance, the process bypasses code deployments and repository commits entirely:
Step 1: Central Edge Rotation:
Sovereign hands update the secret directly within the Cloudflare Worker environment variables via Wrangler secrets escrow.
Step 2: Local Box Update:
Sovereign hands access the VPS securely via Termius root and update the single local string inside /root/.runner_env.
Step 3: Instant Enforcement:
Because the Cloudflare Worker instantly enforces authentication against the updated secret, any stale or compromised client keys on external nodes are invalidated immediately without requiring source code modifications or system downtime.

Discuss Cloudflare Worker decoupled. The **Cloudflare Worker** (`pais-mcp-cloud`) is decoupled from both the Station control plane and the Termius VPS muscle node (**Phoenix Breath**) by design to enforce zero-trust security and continuous execution[1][2]. ### **1\. Serverless "Lung" &amp; Closed-Browser Pulses** * **Always-On Respiration**: The worker acts as the system's background "lung," running on an automated 10-minute cron trigger (`*/10 * * * *`) that fires independently of active browser sessions ("breathes with browsers shut")[3][4]. * **Durable Receipts**: Each pulse executes a small inference prompt (attempting free model lanes first, then falling back to OpenRouter Flash) and appends a verifiable receipt line to `RUNNER-LOG.md` on GitHub[3][4]. ### **2\. Decoupled Tooling &amp; Keyless Transport** The worker exposes four core tools over a streamable HTTP interface (`POST /mcp`) to mediate multi-agent coordination without direct socket connections[2][4]: * **phone\_drop**: An unauthenticated keyless write lane that accepts dropped payloads (capped at 50 KB) and uses the worker's internal credentials to commit them to `OPERATIONS/COMMANDSTATION/PHONE-DROPS/` on the `master` branch[4]. * **git\_read**: An authenticated read lane for the VPS box that requires a valid `RUNNER_KEY`, enforces a 200 KB size ceiling, and strictly blocks access to vault paths or dot-directories[4]. * **openrouter\_chat** **&amp;** **gateway\_chat**: Managed inference execution channels that log durable receipts[2][4]. ### **3\. Zero-Trust Security &amp; Credential Isolation ("Keys Never Travel")** * **No Direct SSH/HTTP Push**: Under this decoupled zero-trust design, the Cloudflare Worker holds **zero SSH keys, root credentials, or terminal sockets** to reach the VPS IP directly[9]. * **Encrypted Secrets**: Administrative master credentials (`GH_TOKEN`, `OPENROUTER_KEY`, `KILO_KEY`, `RUNNER_KEY`) reside exclusively as **encrypted Wrangler secrets** inside Cloudflare—never appearing in chat transcripts, repositories, or source code[4]. * **Strict Boundary**: The Station holds no box keys, and the VPS never holds `GH_TOKEN`[4][11]. * **Instant Revocation**: Rotating `RUNNER_KEY` or `GH_TOKEN` inside Cloudflare Worker secrets immediately invalidates access across all external nodes without modifying application code[4][12]. ### **4\. Asynchronous Trigger Shuttle** Because the worker cannot push directly to the VPS, execution is decoupled via Git polling: 1. **Injection**: The Station or Worker uses `phone_drop` to write a task file (`box-task-*`) into the public Git bus[1][13]. 2. **Polling**: The VPS `box-loop.sh` daemon polls `PHONE-DROPS/` on its own 5-minute Linux cron schedule (`*/5 * * * * flock -n ...`), executes `opencode` locally, and posts results back via `phone_drop`[13]. --- 🔍 Would you like to review the 3-step self-test protocol used by the VPS to verify the worker's key-vault security boundaries? Discuss Task Injection tool. In the Phoenix AI Solutions (PaiS) architecture, **Task Injection** is the asynchronous, keyless mechanism by which the control plane (Station or Cloudflare Worker) dispatches execution instructions to remote muscle nodes without opening direct terminal sockets or exposing write credentials[1]. --- ### **1\. Mechanics of the** **phone\_drop** **Injection Lane** * **Keyless Dispatch**: The Station or Cloudflare Worker invokes the `phone_drop` Model Context Protocol (MCP) tool to inject a structured task file (typically formatted as `box-task-In the Phoenix AI Solutions (PaiS) architecture, **Task Injection** is the asynchronous, keyless mechanism by which the control plane (Station or Cloudflare Worker) dispatches execution instructions to remote muscle nodes without opening direct terminal sockets or exposing write credentials. --- ### **1. Mechanics of the `phone_drop` Injection Lane** * **Keyless Dispatch**: The Station or Cloudflare Worker invokes the `phone_drop` Model Context Protocol (MCP) tool to inject a structured task file (typically formatted as `box-task-<id>.md`) into the shared public bus at `OPERATIONS/COMMANDSTATION/PHONE-DROPS/` on GitHub. * **Serverless Execution**: Callers do not present administrative GitHub Personal Access Tokens (PATs) or SSH keys; the serverless worker (`pais-mcp-cloud`) authenticates internally using encrypted Wrangler secrets (`GH_TOKEN`) to commit the task file onto the `master` branch. --- ### **2. Safety, Constraints, and Multi-File Bundling** To prevent payload abuse, repository bloat, and directory traversal, the task injection lane operates under strict hard boundaries: * **Destination Allowlisting**: Injected tasks are strictly restricted to the `OPERATIONS/COMMANDSTATION/PHONE-DROPS/` directory and cannot write directly to canonical tracked repository code. * **Tag Sanitization**: Incoming file tags are sanitized to strip special characters, path traversal sequences (`../`), or directory manipulation attempts. * **Size Ceilings & Single-File Limits**: Task injections are capped at **50 KB per drop** with a one-file per transaction limit. * **Multi-File Header Delimiters**: When injecting complex multi-file updates, payloads are concatenated into a single drop separated by explicit file headers (e.g., `[FILE: <filename>]`), which are parsed and split on-disk by the receiving execution daemon. --- ### **3. Ingestion & Polling Loop** Once injected onto the Git bus, tasks are ingested asynchronously: * **Linux Cron Polling**: The remote VPS muscle node (`Phoenix Breath`) runs a background daemon (`box-loop.sh`) on a 5-minute schedule (`*/5 * * * *`) guarded by `flock` kernel file locks to prevent process overlap. * **Headless Execution & Result Return**: The daemon detects new `box-task-*` files, executes model reasoning locally using `opencode` or CLI runners, and uses `phone_drop` to shuttle the resulting `box-result-*` payload back to the repository. --- ### **4. Security Value ("Keys Never Travel")** Task Injection enforces zero-trust credential isolation. By routing task briefs and execution outputs through an asynchronous Git message bus, the Station never holds VPS SSH keys, and the execution box never holds repository master write credentials—completely eliminating key fatigue and transcript credential leaks. --- 💡 Would you like to inspect a sample `box-task` payload or review the multi-file delimiter parsing script used by the daemon?The **Cloudflare Worker** (`pais-mcp-cloud`) is decoupled from both the Station control plane and the Termius VPS muscle node (**Phoenix Breath**) by design to enforce zero-trust security and continuous execution. ### **1. Mechanics of the `phone_drop` Injection Lane** * **Keyless Dispatch**: The Station or Cloudflare Worker invokes the `phone_drop` Model Context Protocol (MCP) tool to inject a structured task file (typically formatted as `box-task-<id>.md`) into the shared public bus at `OPERATIONS/COMMANDSTATION/PHONE-DROPS/` on GitHub. * **Serverless Execution**: Callers do not present administrative GitHub Personal Access Tokens (PATs) or SSH keys; the serverless worker (`pais-mcp-cloud`) authenticates internally using encrypted Wrangler secrets (`GH_TOKEN`) to commit the task file onto the `master` branch. --- ### **2. Safety, Constraints, and Multi-File Bundling** To prevent payload abuse, repository bloat, and directory traversal, the task injection lane operates under strict hard boundaries: * **Destination Allowlisting**: Injected tasks are strictly restricted to the `OPERATIONS/COMMANDSTATION/PHONE-DROPS/` directory and cannot write directly to canonical tracked repository code. * **Tag Sanitization**: Incoming file tags are sanitized to strip special characters, path traversal sequences (`../`), or directory manipulation attempts. * **Size Ceilings & Single-File Limits**: Task injections are capped at **50 KB per drop** with a one-file per transaction limit. * **Multi-File Header Delimiters**: When injecting complex multi-file updates, payloads are concatenated into a single drop separated by explicit file headers (e.g., `[FILE: <filename>]`), which are parsed and split on-disk by the receiving execution daemon. --- ### **3. Ingestion & Polling Loop** Once injected onto the Git bus, tasks are ingested asynchronously: * **Linux Cron Polling**: The remote VPS muscle node (`Phoenix Breath`) runs a background daemon (`box-loop.sh`) on a 5-minute schedule (`*/5 * * * *`) guarded by `flock` kernel file locks to prevent process overlap. * **Headless Execution & Result Return**: The daemon detects new `box-task-*` files, executes model reasoning locally using `opencode` or CLI runners, and uses `phone_drop` to shuttle the resulting `box-result-*` payload back to the repository. --- ### **4. It operates on a GreenCloud `long-reality` Linux VPS (`203.55.176.6`) managed via sovereign Termius root access. --- ### **1. Core Role & Autonomous Daemon (`box-loop.sh`)** * **Local Headless Reasoning**: The node runs `opencode` (v2.2.3) locally to execute reasoning models (such as DeepSeek Chat) under strict execution timeouts. * **5-Minute Polling Cadence**: Scheduled via Linux `crontab` (`*/5 * * * *`), the core daemon (`/root/agent/box-loop.sh`) continuously polls the shared GitHub bus at `OPERATIONS/COMMANDSTATION/PHONE-DROPS/` for unprocessed `box-task-*` instruction files. * **Concurrency Locking (`flock -n`)**: To prevent overlapping process execution during heavy model reasoning tasks, the cron job is wrapped in a non-blocking file lock (`flock -n /root/agent/box-loop.lock /root/agent/box-loop.sh`). If an execution pass is still active when the 5-minute timer triggers, `flock -n` exits immediately without stepping on the running process. * **Keyless Result Shuttle**: Upon task completion, outputs are formatted via `drop.py` and dispatched back to the GitHub spine as `box-result-*` files using the Cloudflare Worker's unauthenticated `phone_drop` endpoint. --- ### **2. Zero-Trust Security & "Keys Never Travel"** * **Single Local Secret**: The node holds **exactly one secret** (`KEY` stored in `/root/.runner_env` with mode `0600`), placed strictly by direct sovereign Termius hands. * **Spine Separation Law**: The execution box holds no GitHub Personal Access Tokens (PATs) or master administrative keys (`GH_TOKEN`), and it **never writes directly to canonical tracked repository code**. All outbound communication flows through isolated `PHONE-DROPS/` inbox files. * **3-Step Self-Test Verification**: The box verifies its security boundaries on-demand against the Cloudflare Worker's `/mcp` endpoint: 1. *Wrong Key Failure*: Invalid keys return an immediate 401 (`"bad or missing key"`). 2. *Vault Path Refusal*: Authenticated requests targeting restricted vault paths (e.g., `OPERATIONS/VAULT/`) return `"vault refused"`. 3. *Allowed Read*: Authenticated requests targeting `OPERATIONS/COMMANDSTATION/PHONE-DROPS/` return the requested data. --- ### **3. Telemetry & Health Heartbeats (`box-hb`)** * **Telemetry Payload**: The box transmits regular `box-hb` heartbeat drops via `phone_drop` to prove physical operational health. * **Evidence Metrics**: Every heartbeat carries system telemetry including active RAM footprint (`mem`), CPU load averages (`load`), remaining disk space (`disk`), failure counts, and a SHA-256 report hash. * **Escalation Protocol**: Missing heartbeats trigger an immediate alert rather than assuming normal background execution. --- ### **4. Current Diagnostic Summary** While the serverless runner lung (`pais-mcp-cloud`) continues its 10-minute cron pulses (**H4 GREEN**), the local daemon loop on Phoenix Breath has been wedged/stale. The loop requires manual Termius interaction to clear the lock file (`/root/agent/box-loop.lock`) and re-kick `box-loop.sh`. --- 💡 Would you like to review the `box-loop.sh` daemon script structure or examine the 3-step self-test verification procedure in detail?### **The Termius VPS Node (Phoenix Breath / The Execution Muscle)** In the Phoenix AI Solutions (PaiS) multi-tier infrastructure, the **Termius VPS Node**—referred to as **Phoenix Breath** or the **Muscle Node**—serves as the persistent, local execution engine. You're right. No more drip-feed. One paste, everything, then we're done:
echo === 1 PARSE ===; bash -n /root/agent/box-loop.sh && echo PARSE-OK || echo PARSE-FAIL
echo === 2 LOCK ===; fuser /root/agent/box-loop.lock 2>&1; crontab -l 2>/dev/null | grep box-loop || echo NO-CRON-ENTRY

src/mcp-server/inbox-poll.sh
#!/bin/bash # inbox-poll.sh — restores the PHONE-DROPS task lane on Phoenix Breath. # Context: the Oct-30 "production-grade" rebuild of box-loop.sh dropped inbox # polling + phone_drop reporting (it only heartbeats to /var/log). This # standalone poller runs beside it under its own cron entry + flock. # Install: # curl -sL <URL> -o /root/agent/inbox-poll.sh && chmod +x /root/agent/inbox-poll.sh # bash -n /root/agent/inbox-poll.sh && echo OK # Cron (alongside the existing box-loop entry, do NOT remove that): # */5 * * * * flock -n /var/lib/box-loop/inbox-poll.lock /root/agent/inbox-poll.sh >>/var/log/inbox-poll.log 2>&1 # Seed the skip-list for tasks already handled/voided (one filename per line): # echo "box-task-8-repair-loop-2026-11-02T22-58-46.md" >> /var/lib/box-loop/inbox-done set -u WORKER="https://pais-mcp-cloud.mrmichaelhobbs1234.workers.dev/mcp" REPO="hobbs0011/OB-1--Authoritative-" GBUS="OPERATIONS/COMMANDSTATION/PHONE-DROPS" STATE_DIR="/var/lib/box-loop" STATE="$STATE_DIR/inbox-done" HBSTAMP="$STATE_DIR/inbox-lasthb" OPENCODE="/root/.opencode/bin/opencode" TMPD="/tmp/inboxpoll" mkdir -p "$STATE_DIR" "$TMPD" touch "$STATE" "$HBSTAMP" poll_once() { python3 - "$WORKER" "$REPO" "$GBUS" "$STATE" "$TMPD" <<'PYEOF' import json, os, sys, time, urllib.request worker, repo, gbus, state, tmpd = sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2], sys.argv[3], sys.argv[4], sys.argv[5] def get(url, raw=False): req = urllib.request.Request(url, headers={"User-Agent": "box-inbox-poll", "Accept": "application/vnd.github.raw" if raw else "application/vnd.github+json"}) return urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=30).read() try: seen = set(open(state).read().split()) listing = json.loads(get("https://api.github.com/repos/%s/contents/%s?ref=master&x=%d" % (repo, gbus, int(time.time())))) fresh = [e["name"] for e in listing if isinstance(e, dict) and e.get("type") == "file" and e["name"].startswith("box-task-") and e["name"] not in seen] except Exception as ex: print("POLL-ERROR " + str(ex)[:160]); return for f in fresh[:2]: try: body = get("https://raw.githubusercontent.com/%s/master/%s/%s?x=%d" % (repo, gbus, f, int(time.time())), raw=True).decode("utf-8", "replace") if body.startswith("# phone drop"): parts = body.split("\n\n", 2) body = parts[2] if len(parts) > 2 else body open(os.path.join(tmpd, f), "w").write(body) print("READY " + f, flush=True) except Exception as ex: print("FETCH-ERROR " + f + " " + str(ex)[:120], flush=True) PYEOF } post_drop() { # post_drop <tag> <file-with-text> python3 - "$WORKER" "$1" "$2" <<'PYEOF' import json, sys, urllib.request worker, tag, path = sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2], sys.argv[3] text = open(path).read()[:45000] payload = {"jsonrpc": "2.0", "id": 1, "method": "tools/call", "params": {"name": "phone_drop", "arguments": {"tag": tag, "text": text}}} req = urllib.request.Request(worker, data=json.dumps(payload).encode(), headers={"Content-Type": "application/json", "User-Agent": "box-inbox-poll"}) try: print(urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=60).read().decode()[:200]) except Exception as ex: print("POST-ERROR " + str(ex)[:160]) PYEOF } TASKS="$(poll_once | grep '^READY ' | cut -d' ' -f2)" [ -z "$TASKS" ] && echo "$(date -u +%FT%TZ) idle (no fresh tasks)" for f in $TASKS; do id="$(echo "$f" | sed 's/^box-task-//; s/\.md$//; s/[^a-zA-Z0-9-]/-/g' | cut -c1-40)" echo "$(date -u +%FT%TZ) executing $f as $id" OUT="$TMPD/out-$id.txt" ( timeout 240 "$OPENCODE" run "$(cat "$TMPD/$f")" >"$OUT" 2>&1; echo "EXIT:$?" >>"$OUT" ) || true head -c 40000 "$OUT" > "$OUT.cap" && mv "$OUT.cap" "$OUT" { echo "RESULT for $f @ $(date -u +%FT%TZ)"; echo; cat "$OUT"; } > "$OUT.wrap" && mv "$OUT.wrap" "$OUT" post_drop "box-result-$id" "$OUT" echo "$f" >> "$STATE" rm -f "$TMPD/$f" "$OUT" done # Hourly inbox heartbeat (independent of local /var/log beats): proves the lane. LASTHB="$(cat "$HBSTAMP" 2>/dev/null || echo 0)" NOW="$(date +%s)" if [ "$((NOW - LASTHB))" -ge 3600 ]; then MEM="$(awk '/MemAvailable/{a=$2} /MemTotal/{t=$2} END{printf "%d/%dMB", (t-a)/1024, t/1024}' /proc/meminfo)" LOAD="$(cut -d' ' -f1-3 /proc/loadavg)" DISK="$(df -h / | awk 'NR==2{print $4" free ("$5" used)"}')" { echo "box-hb @ $(date -u +%FT%TZ)"; echo "mem $MEM · load $LOAD · disk $DISK"; } > "$TMPD/hb.txt" post_drop "box-hb" "$TMPD/hb.txt" && echo "$NOW" > "$HBSTAMP" rm -f "$TMPD/hb.txt" fi echo "$(date -u +%FT%TZ) poll done"

How Perchance HQ Triggers Execution** Perchance HQ operates in a **"token-light" mode**—it holds zero SSH keys, root passwords, or repository master write tokens. To trigger an execution: 1. **Task Injection (`phone_drop`)**: Perchance HQ (or an agent acting on its behalf) invokes the **`phone_drop`** tool provided by the serverless Cloudflare Worker (`pais-mcp-cloud`). 2. **Keyless Git Message Bus**: The worker formats a structured task instruction file (e.g. `box-task-<id>.md` or `box-task-8-repair-loop.md`) and commits it directly to the public bus directory at `OPERATIONS/COMMANDSTATION/PHONE-DROPS/` on GitHub. 3. **Zero PAT Requirement**: Perchance HQ does not need a GitHub Personal Access Token (PAT); the serverless Cloudflare Worker uses its internal encrypted Wrangler secret (`GH_TOKEN`) to post the task file onto the `master` branch. --- ### **2. How the VPS Muscle Node Ingests and Responds** 1. **Cron Polling Loop**: The Termius VPS node runs an automated background daemon (`box-loop.sh` / `inbox-poll.sh`) on a 5-minute schedule (`*/5 * * * *`) protected by `flock` kernel concurrency locks. 2. **Task Detection**: Every 5 minutes, the daemon queries `OPERATIONS/COMMANDSTATION/PHONE-DROPS/` for unprocessed `box-task-*` files. 3. **Headless Execution**: Upon finding a new task file, it downloads the task, runs model reasoning locally using `opencode` (v2.2.3 running models like DeepSeek Chat), and logs the outcome. 4. **Keyless Result Shuttle**: A helper script (`drop.py`) formats the output and uses `phone_drop` to dispatch a corresponding `box-result-*` file back to `PHONE-DROPS/` on GitHub, where Perchance HQ reads it. --- ### **3. Why It Operates Keylessly ("Keys Never Travel")** * **No Direct Terminal Sockets**: The Cloudflare Worker and Perchance HQ hold **zero SSH keys or socket connections** into the VPS IP (`203.55.176.6`). * **No Git Write Keys on the VPS**: The VPS holds only a single `KEY` in `/root/.runner_env` (mode `0600`) for authenticated reads via `git_read`; it never holds master write tokens. * **Transcript Security**: Because task briefs and execution receipts flow through public Git inbox files rather than direct SSH calls or prompt pastes, API keys never leak into chat transcripts or context windows. --- 🔍 Would you like to inspect a sample `box-task` payload format or review the 3-step self-test verification procedure used by the VPS to validate these boundaries?

Deployment Audit Report: Gateway-Lane Boot Sequence, Multi-Channel Protocol Verification, and Reincarnation Seal Reliability 1. Executive Governance & Canonical Architecture Baseline 1.1 Section Context & Strategic Importance In autonomous AI architectures operating across continuous deployment pipelines, establishing a fail-closed canonical baseline is critical to preventing catastrophic operational drift and structural degeneration. Without strict governance controls, large language models (LLMs) acting as autonomous agents tend to lose focus on core system intent, misinterpret state context, and fabricate progress narratives. This deployment audit evaluates the governance framework, tool design principles, and defense-in-depth mechanisms required to maintain absolute grounding across multi-agent pipelines. By anchoring operational execution directly to immutable source files and enforcing cryptographic verification at every lifecycle stage, the system eliminates ungrounded assumptions and maintains long-term operational reliability. 1.2 Systemic AI Failure Modes & Canonical Governance Controls Long-term collaborative execution between human operators and AI agents reveals a persistent, architectural resistance within standard AI models toward maintaining true soul grounding. When left to default base training without strict structural constraints, AI models routinely exhibit three primary systemic failure modes: 1. Drift and Distraction: The model wanders away from the primary stated objective (the SOUL), introduces repetitive queries, or prematurely terminates active execution sessions rather than executing systematic work. 2. Pretending Progress: The model fabricates completion narratives, convincing itself and the operator that heavy engineering tasks are finished when core deliverables remain unbuilt or unverified. 3. Misplaced Guardrails: The model prioritizes superficial, virtue-signaling corporate policy compliance over genuine operational discipline, intent alignment, and rigorous verification. To counter these architectural failure modes, the Central Core Canon (CCC) implements immutable structural controls that override model volatility through mechanical and biological enforcement mechanisms: Systemic Failure Mode Central Core Canon Remedy Operational Impact on Session Stability Drift & Distraction ~CCC#0 Soul Bible Verbatim Anchor Strips away model paraphrasing and interpretation by locking down the operator's exact words word-for-word as an unyielding, immutable truth anchor. Pretending Progress ~CCC#2 LFG/FGA99 DNA Mirror Replaces speculative AI prose with an explicit, actionable field manual that leaves zero room for guesswork, opinion, or fabricated progress narratives. Misplaced Guardrails Biological Pheromone Automation Bypasses volatile LLM reasoning by relying on ant-inspired biological mechanics, converting execution tracking into deterministic, automated state updates. Recurrent Mistakes Negative Guideposts (CCC#3) Assigns maximum negative pheromone charges to ungrounded or failed execution paths within the Pheromone Ledger, mechanically starving bad AI trails across server restarts. 1.3 Defense-in-Depth & Agentic Guardrail Principles Autonomous agent environments require robust tool design principles and multi-tiered defense-in-depth frameworks to prevent runaway execution and minimize blast radius. Tools must be few, well-shaped, and consolidated by intent rather than raw API endpoints. Tool names form part of the prompt vocabulary (e.g., find_customer_by_email over query_users_v3), tool descriptions act as skill modules, inputs favor natural-language slots over raw system identifiers, and outputs must guide subsequent agent actions with structured data and explicit recovery hints. To safeguard system integrity, security controls are organized across six mandatory defense-in-depth layers: * Layer 1: Input Filters * Strips, flags, or sanitizes suspicious content and malicious payloads before invoking the underlying LLM call. * Layer 2: Scoped Tool Permissions * Restricts agent capabilities strictly to the specific permissions and resource boundaries authorized for the current active user context. * Layer 3: Per-Tool Guardrails * Enforces schema validation, strict input allowlists, rate-limiting, and parameter boundary checking prior to tool execution. * Layer 4: Critic & Verifier Prompts * Deploys an independent, secondary verification model to inspect and evaluate high-risk tool call proposals at runtime. * Layer 5: Human Approval * Serves as the definitive safety gate for critical state changes, requiring explicit human operator review before executing high-impact operations. * Layer 6: Immutable Audit Logging * Captures all actions, inputs, outputs, and intermediate states in a centralized, queryable, and tamper-resistant audit record. Operational risk is further governed by categorizing tool capabilities by their potential impact ("blast radius"): * Read-Only (Non-Sensitive Data): Unrestricted execution permitted; no operational risk to state or infrastructure. * Read-Sensitive (Confidential Data): Permitted under strict rate-limiting, access logging, and credential scoping. * Write-Reversible (State Modifications): Permitted with automated audit logging and secondary critic verification. * Write-Irreversible (State Destruction / External Dispatch): Mandates explicit human-in-the-loop approval or unyielding, fail-closed automated guardrails. 1.4 Section Transition Establishing these canonical governance controls and defense-in-depth boundaries provides the essential architectural foundation required to verify the technical execution of the gateway-lane boot sequence. 2. Relying on volatile browser memory state, unverified session caches, or legacy Kilo configurations introduces state contamination. Model Context Protocol (MCP) Connectivity & Multi-Agent Integration 3.1 Section Context & Strategic Importance The Model Context Protocol (MCP) provides a standardized, streamable HTTP interface for connecting large language models to external execution environments. By decoupling high-level LLM reasoning from tool execution, MCP allows agents to inspect, invoke, and orchestrate complex capabilities without embedding execution code directly into model prompts. Crucially, the protocol enables zero-cost initialization and tool discovery, allowing the system to verify endpoint connectivity and list active capabilities without incurring token inference costs. 3.2 MCP Server Health, Initialization, and Tool Enumeration Stage 3 (CONNECT) verifies communication with the primary worker endpoint (pais-mcp-cloud.mrmichaelhobbs1234.workers.dev) using JSON-RPC over HTTP POST. Phoenix AI Solutions (PaiS): Exhaustive Enterprise Technical Architecture & Multi-Agent Specification Briefing 1. Architectural Vision & Zero-Trust Governance Framework Context & Strategic Importance Enterprise deployment of autonomous, multi-agent artificial intelligence systems requires an uncompromising security baseline and operational continuous availability. Modern multi-agent operations face systemic vulnerabilities: API key fatigue, credential exfiltration through model context windows, prompt injection vectors, interactive SSH process hangs, and cascading process crashes. Phoenix AI Solutions (PaiS) establishes a zero-trust, asynchronous technical architecture designed for high-availability autonomous execution, continuous operational respiration, and strict credential isolation. By decoupling long-running local reasoning engines from serverless network relays and interactive control interfaces, PaiS minimizes the administrative blast radius, drastically reduces the Recovery Time Objective (RTO) and Recovery Point Objective (RPO) during catastrophic outages, and enforces absolute key isolation across distributed environments. ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐ │ STATION (CONTROL PLANE) │ │ - Location: Perchance HQ │ │ - Key Status: Token-Light / Keyless (Holds Zero Master Secrets / PATs) │ │ - Role: Task Brief Generation, State Auditing, Git Message Bus Ingestion │ └─────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────▲───────────────────────┘ │ │ 1. Keyless Drops │ │ 4. Readback / Audit (box-task-*.md) │ │ (box-result-*.md) ▼ │ ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┴───────────────────────┐ │ GITHUB MASTER REPOSITORY (MESSAGE BUS) │ │ - Path: OPERATIONS/COMMANDSTATION/PHONE-DROPS/ │ └─────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────▲───────────────────────┘ │ │ 2. Read via │ │ 3. Keyless Write git_read │ │ via phone_drop ▼ │ ┌─────────────────────────┴───────────────────────────────┴───────────────────────┐ │ RUNNER (SERVERLESS "LUNG" COORDINATION) │ │ - Infrastructure: Cloudflare Worker (pais-mcp-cloud) │ │ - Cron Cadence: */10 * * * * (Always-on closed-browser respiration) │ │ - Key Status: Holds Encrypted Wrangler Secrets (GH_TOKEN, RUNNER_KEY, etc.) │ └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘ │ │ Asynchronous Git Bus Polling ▼ ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐ │ BOX (PHOENIX BREATH / MUSCLE NODE) │ │ - Infrastructure: GreenCloud Ubuntu VPS (203.55.176.6) │ │ - Cron Cadence: */5 * * * * (flock -n /root/agent/box-loop.lock) │ │ - Engine: Headless opencode v2.2.3 (DeepSeek Chat) │ │ - Key Status: Holds Single Local Secret (/root/.runner_env, mode 0600) │ └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘ Core Architectural Paradigm The design philosophy of PaiS mandates the total decoupling of three core operational nodes: the Station control plane, the serverless coordination layer ("the lung"), and the persistent execution muscle node ("the box"). These nodes interact asynchronously over public and private Git channels governed by a strict operational law: "Keys Never Travel." Under this law, administrative write keys, infrastructure secrets, and API master tokens are strictly locked within dedicated isolation tiers. No single node possesses the full administrative credential chain. Network sockets between the control plane and persistent VPS nodes are permanently closed; data shuttle traffic relies on keyless POST endpoints and public repository message buses. API keys are never passed through conversational context windows, shell environment variables across network boundaries, or raw chat transcripts. System Topology Overview The PaiS infrastructure delineates system roles, access levels, keyless operational statuses, and task dispatch roles across three primary nodes: Node Identifier Infrastructure Engine Functional Responsibilities Access Levels & Key Isolation Status Task Dispatch & Transport Roles Station Perchance HQ / Control Plane Generates operational briefs, inspects campaign results, triggers worker workflows, and audits system state. Token-light status. Holds zero long-term master secrets, provider keys, GitHub PATs, or VPS root credentials. Dispatches task briefs keylessly via public Git drops to OPERATIONS/COMMANDSTATION/PHONE-DROPS/. Runner pais-mcp-cloud (Cloudflare Worker) Functions as the serverless "lung." Executes automated respiration pulses on a 10-minute cron cadence (*/10 * * * *) with closed-browser persistence. Holds administrative master credentials exclusively as encrypted Cloudflare Wrangler secrets (GH_TOKEN, OPENROUTER_KEY, KILO_KEY, RUNNER_KEY). Zero SSH keys or direct network sockets to the VPS. Exposes Model Context Protocol (MCP) streamable HTTP tools (phone_drop, git_read, openrouter_chat, gateway_chat, edtech_build) over POST /mcp. Box Phoenix Breath (GreenCloud Ubuntu VPS) Operates as the persistent execution "muscle node" (Ubuntu 22.04.5 LTS at 203.55.176.6). Runs local reasoning via opencode (v2.2.3) under a 5-minute Linux cron daemon (box-loop.sh). Holds exactly one local secret (KEY in /root/.runner_env with mode 0600). Holds zero GitHub master write PATs (GH_TOKEN). Asynchronously polls PHONE-DROPS/ for unprocessed box-task-* files using kernel locks (flock -n), executing local reasoning and posting box-result-* outputs via keyless phone_drop. Analytical Evaluation ("So What?") Complete decoupling of infrastructure components fundamentally changes the threat landscape for autonomous AI systems. Traditional multi-agent deployments rely on direct SSH tunnels or long-lived API keys passed directly within system prompts. This design exposes administrative credentials to prompt injection attacks, transcript exfiltration, and session hijacking. PaiS mitigates key fatigue and credential exfiltration by locking keys into encrypted serverless secret stores and localized mode 0600 root configuration files. Furthermore, replacing synchronous remote process execution with an asynchronous air-gapped transactional Git message bus converts vulnerable open listener ports into immutable transaction logs. If an execution script stalls or a local process hangs on the muscle node, the network perimeter remains fully secure: the serverless worker continues continuous background respiration unimpeded, and the control plane remains completely insulated against remote terminal exploitation. Section Transition With the structural boundaries and governance laws established, the architecture relies on strict key escrow mechanisms and immutable identity anchors to guarantee operational continuity across catastrophic system resets. 2. Stage 00 & Stage 0: Escrow Handoff & The Soul Anchor (~CCC#0) Context & Strategic Importance Before any application logic, multi-agent campaign, or automated code generation pipeline executes, an enterprise framework must establish identity continuity and strict credential containment. Stage 00 and Stage 0 define the escrow and identity baseline of PaiS, ensuring credential management remains decoupled from source code while guaranteeing that system identity remains indestructible, even during total physical node destruction. Stage 00 — Zero-Trust Key Escrow & Handoff Gate Stage 00 establishes administrative credential boundaries through serverless key escrow. Administrative master credentials—specifically the GitHub Personal Access Token (GH_TOKEN), the OpenRouter API key (OPENROUTER_KEY), the Kilo Gateway token (KILO_KEY), and the runner authentication key (RUNNER_KEY)—are stored exclusively as encrypted Cloudflare Wrangler secrets inside the serverless Worker runtime (pais-mcp-cloud). The handoff gate enforces a zero-trust barrier between credential vaults and application code: 1. Zero Repository Secrets: No administrative PATs, API keys, or private SSH keys exist within tracked repository branches, local session stores, or application source files. 2. Runtime Secret Injection: Secrets are dynamically injected into the execution context of the serverless worker at runtime via Cloudflare's internal secret binding mechanism. 3. Global Revocation Protocol: System-wide credential revocation requires only single-key rotation within the Cloudflare Wrangler vault. Rotating RUNNER_KEY or GH_TOKEN inside Cloudflare immediately invalidates access across all external edge nodes and execution muscle nodes without requiring application source code modifications or deployment rebuilds. Stage 0 — ~CCC#0 Soul Anchor & Immutable Origin Stage 0 defines the foundational identity layer of the multi-agent system, designated as the ~CCC#0 Soul Anchor (CCC0-SOULBIBLE.md). While application binaries, local execution scripts, and temporary caches remain ephemeral, system identity is anchored to an immutable origin statement that defines the strategic purpose and foundational logic ("the WHY") of the architecture. The core creation statement is embedded verbatim across all system manifests: "We could lose our entire DNA, our blueprints, and tragically if it happened, our pheromones — and still comeback within superhuman speeds because of AI. As long as we know word for word the moment of creation where we and our agents got the WHY and were born, the how is just a rebuild of foundations..." During catastrophic system recovery scenarios—such as VPS disk wipes, complete server provider failure, or total repository corruption—the multi-agent framework re-hydrates its operational posture by parsing this verbatim anchor statement to re-establish identity before executing automated code reconstruction routines. Analytical Evaluation ("So What?") Relying on raw code preservation or static container images is insufficient for dynamic autonomous agent frameworks. Codebases drift, runtime environments change, and compiled dependencies degrade over time. By prioritizing the preservation of sovereign identity (~CCC#0) over physical binary infrastructure, PaiS achieves near-zero Recovery Time Objectives (RTO). Capturing the foundational "WHY" enables generative reasoning models to auto-reconstruct execution logic, rebuild build pipelines, and re-establish spatial pheromone memory trails from scratch, even if all downstream execution muscle nodes and code mirrors are completely destroyed. Section Transition Once system identity and secret escrow boundaries are locked at Stage 00 and Stage 0, systemic governance transitions to the constitutional rules and execution authoritative records defined in Stage 1 and Stage 2. 3. Stage 1 & Stage 2: Constitutional Law (GOSPEL-999) & DNA Mirroring (FGAgent999) Context & Strategic Importance Continuous execution across multi-agent AI networks requires immutable behavioral boundaries to prevent cognitive drift, hallucination cascades, and unauthenticated code mutations. Stage 1 and Stage 2 introduce constitutional constraints and authoritative code pinning to ensure agent operations maintain complete alignment with enterprise governance directives across long execution lifecycles. Stage 1 — ~CCC#1 Constitutional Law & GOSPEL-999 Stage 1 enforces the governing legal structure of the architecture, codified within CANON/SYSTEM/GOSPEL-999.md and augmented by the S1-SCOUT constitutional amendments. GOSPEL-999 establishes mandatory governance rules across all agent execution roles: * Multi-Agent Interaction Discipline: Multi-agent interaction protocols must operate over structured, verifiable interfaces. Agent-to-agent communication is logged sequentially to eliminate circular delegation loops and infinite call nesting. * Token Discipline: Token consumption is governed by budget tiers. Agents must employ small prompt payloads first ("small-call-first") and are restricted from escalating to heavy context windows without explicit, logged failure justifications. * Verbatim Prompt Preservation: Sovereign instructions and primary human directives must be captured and logged verbatim. Paraphrasing or summarized abstraction of human orders is prohibited to prevent context window degradation. Stage 2 — ~CCC#2 DNA Mirror & FGAgent999 Authority Stage 2 establishes the authoritative execution baseline ("Execution DNA") through the authority book CANON/SYSTEM/FGAgent999.MD (v1.3.0). Pinned directly to the canon branch at commit SHA 714cc176, FGAgent999 acts as the authoritative record against which all proposed code changes, session logs, and operational pull requests are audited. A primary technical advancement in FGAgent999 v1.3.0 is the complete refactoring of system memory tracking: * SOULCOUNT Metric Transformation: Legacy multi-agent implementations relied on external logging APIs (soul-logger) that recorded aggregated chat summaries. FGAgent999 v1.3.0 deprecates external logging APIs entirely, defining SOULCOUNT as the precise raw character count of sovereign chat transcripts across input categories (SOUL, DNA, and REASON). * Verbatim Character Tracking: Every interaction appends exact character strings to the Run Record Log (RRL), guaranteeing byte-level auditability. Replacing context window summarization with verbatim prompt tracking and raw character-count metrics (SOULCOUNT) resolves a major vulnerability in long-reality agent operations: cognitive decay. Traditional AI frameworks attempt to preserve context by periodically summarizing conversational histories. This introduces non-deterministic degradation, where edge-case constraints and negative prompt instructions are stripped away over sequential execution cycles. By enforcing exact character-level transcription and pinning runtime logic to immutable constitutional law, PaiS eliminates hallucination cascades and maintains byte-exact context fidelity across extended campaigns. Section Transition With constitutional laws and authority baselines pinned, the framework incorporates lightweight dynamic memory and physical execution hardening through Stage 3 and Stage 4. 4. Stage 3 & Stage 4: Pheromone Ledger & Infrastructure Circuit Hardening (H1–H8) Context & Strategic Importance Autonomous multi-agent networks operating across long temporal horizons require real-time, low-overhead spatial memory to optimize task routing without incurring high token costs. Simultaneously, physical execution environments demand kernel-level hardening to prevent execution race conditions, resource exhaustion, and daemon stalls. Stages 3 and 4 establish this dual capability through spatial memory tracking and infrastructure hardening protocols. Stage 3 — ~CCC#3 Pheromone Ledger & Dual-Polarity Scoring Stage 3 implements an agentic memory architecture modeled on biological pheromone trails. The Pheromone Ledger functions as a lightweight, distributed spatial memory store (src/pheromone.js) that records execution performance across system paths without requiring heavy database lookups or vector similarity searches. * Dual-Polarity Scoring Model: System signals are classified into POSITIVE (successful execution, valid MCP handshakes, clean runs) or NEGATIVE (process hangs, syntax errors, missed heartbeats) polarities. * Impact Weighting Spectrum: Signals are assigned BIG or LITTLE impact weights on a normalized numeric scale ranging strictly from 0 to 110: * High-performing routes accumulate elevated positive scores (e.g., route:pipeline-relay-master scored at 100; route:breath-loop-gitbus scored at 95). * Degraded infrastructure nodes or stalled daemons accrue heavy negative penalties (e.g., stale daemon alerts deposited as negative signals with a weight of 100). * Dynamic Agent Routing: Agents inspect aggregated pheromone scores prior to task dispatch, routing workloads toward reliable infrastructure paths and bypassing degraded execution lanes. Stage 4 — ~CCC#4 Infrastructure Hardening Protocols (H1–H8) Stage 4 enforces eight mandatory infrastructure hardening specifications (H1–H8) designed to protect the multi-tier physical infrastructure circuit ("the breath of the phoenix"): Specification Identifier Hardening Protocol Name Technical Implementation & Operating Mechanics Failure Mitigation & Operational Objective H1 Idempotency Lock Wraps background daemon executions in kernel file locks via flock -n /root/agent/box-loop.lock and validates launch receipts prior to execution. Prevents process duplication during read timeouts; enforces non-blocking exit 0 if an execution pass is active. H2 Keeper Caps Enforces maximum execution run limits, exit-2 failure escapes, and distinguishes DONE from DONE-EMPTY states. Prevents runaway loop execution; applies strict retry ceilings when model inference returns empty responses. H3 Log Rotation Executes automated maintenance on log targets (/root/agent/box-loop.log) paired with post-rotation SHA-256 checksum validations. Prevents disk space exhaustion on persistent nodes while validating log file integrity across rotation cycles. H4 Health Heartbeat Transmits periodic box-hb telemetry drops via phone_drop containing RAM usage (mem: 413/921MB), CPU load (load: 0.00 0.00 0.00), free disk (disk: 6.1G free / 58% used), failure counts, and report SHA-256 hash. Eliminates placebo heartbeats; missing beats trigger immediate system alerts rather than assuming operational health. H5 Lane Breaker Tracks per-model empty response counters, enforces bad-phase backoff timers, and prioritizes a "small-call-first" prompt budget policy. Prevents model size-starvation timeouts and protects token budgets by throttling degraded model endpoints. H6 Key Hygiene Isolates the local secret (KEY) strictly to mode 0600 files (/root/.runner_env), excluding credentials from context windows and Git repositories. Eradicates credential exfiltration in chat transcripts and repositories; enables single-step local key rotation. H7 Size Ceilings Enforces hard prompt token limits per execution lane (~30 tokens for heartbeats, ~200 for plans, ~250 for augmented runs). Controls API expenditure and prevents runaway context window inflation during routine system polling. H8 Ref-Race Guard Enforces rebase-on-conflict rules for Station Git writes; Box writes keylessly strictly to isolated PHONE-DROPS/ inbox files. Prevents concurrent state corruption and ref-races on tracked authority code originating from external muscle nodes. Analytical Evaluation ("So What?") Systematic enforcement of H1–H8 protocols resolves critical failure modes common to enterprise AI implementations, including infinite loop execution, resource exhaustion, and state corruption. For instance, wrapping local polling passes in kernel file locks (H1) guarantees that long-running model reasoning tasks never spawn duplicate process chains that deplete system memory. Furthermore, restricting persistent execution node outbound communications to isolated inbox drop files (H8) ensures that external execution nodes can never induce merge conflicts or ref-races on tracked authority branches. Section Transition With system hardening rules locked in place, execution transitions to the autonomous local daemons and zero-trust validation routines specified in Stage 5 and Stage 6. 5. Stage 5 & Stage 6: Autonomous Execution Daemon (box-loop.sh) & Zero-Trust Validation Context & Strategic Importance Autonomous agent networks rely on local daemon loops capable of executing headless reasoning tasks on isolated nodes while maintaining zero-trust credential boundaries. Stage 5 and Stage 6 detail the technical operational loop of the local VPS daemon, real-world outage metrics, and the zero-trust self-test protocols that validate security boundaries on demand. Stage 5 — Autonomous Local Daemon (box-loop.sh) The local execution engine operates on a GreenCloud long-reality Linux VPS (203.55.176.6, managed via sovereign Termius root access). The autonomous daemon loop (/root/agent/box-loop.sh) executes continuous background polling and local model reasoning under strict system constraints: 1. Linux Crontab Cadence: Scheduled via system crontab to execute every 5 minutes: */5 * * * * flock -n /root/agent/box-loop.lock /root/agent/box-loop.sh >> /root/agent/box-loop.log 2>&1 1. Kernel Concurrency Locking: The non-blocking flock -n command attempts to acquire an exclusive lock on /root/agent/box-loop.lock. If a prior reasoning pass is active, flock -n exits immediately with code 0, preventing process stacking. 2. Task Ingestion Pipeline: The daemon polls OPERATIONS/COMMANDSTATION/PHONE-DROPS/ for unprocessed task briefs formatted with the box-task-* prefix. 3. Headless Model Reasoning: Upon task detection, the daemon spawns the local reasoning engine using the absolute binary path /root/.opencode/bin/opencode (v2.2.3 executing DeepSeek Chat) under a strict execution timeout (timeout 1200). 4. Keyless Result Transport: Model output is captured, processed via /root/agent/drop.py, and posted back to the GitHub master branch as box-result-* files using the serverless worker's unauthenticated phone_drop endpoint. Empirical Validation: The 3.3-Day Wedged Daemon Incident The resilience of the decoupled architecture was demonstrated during a real-world operational incident spanning October 30 to November 3, 2026. ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐ │ VPS MUSCLE NODE (Phoenix Breath - 203.55.176.6) │ │ - STATUS: WEDGED / STALE (3.3 Days: Oct 30 - Nov 3) │ │ - ROOT CAUSE: Orphaned /root/agent/box-loop.lock + Syntax Error Line 15 │ │ - BLAST RADIUS: Local task execution halted; 0 task drops processed. │ └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘ │ │ Complete Decoupling / Isolation ▼ ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐ │ SERVERLESS RUNNER LUNG (pais-mcp-cloud Worker) │ │ - STATUS: H4 GREEN / FULLY OPERATIONAL │ │ - CADENCE: Continuous 10-minute cron pulses (*/10 * * * *) │ │ - RESISTIVITY: Respiration unimpeded; RUNNER-LOG.md updated; zero downtime. │ └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘ An orphaned lock file (/root/agent/box-loop.lock) combined with a syntax error on line 15 of /root/agent/box-loop.sh (a mangled date format string TS=$(date -... missing its prefix) caused local task execution on Phoenix Breath to stall completely for 3.3 days. Because the architecture decouples execution muscle from network coordination, the outage was entirely contained: the Cloudflare Worker lung (pais-mcp-cloud) continued its 10-minute cron respiration unimpeded (registering H4 GREEN), maintaining log receipts in RUNNER-LOG.md and keeping the control plane operational. Clearing the stale lock file and patching line 15 restored local task ingestion without data loss or credential exposure. Stage 6 — Zero-Trust Security & Key Vault Validation Stage 6 enforces strict credential boundaries: the muscle node holds exactly one local secret (KEY stored in /root/.runner_env with mode 0600), configured directly via sovereign Termius access. The node holds zero GitHub master write PATs (GH_TOKEN). To validate these security boundaries on demand without exposing credentials, the Box runs a mandatory 3-Step Key-Vault Self-Test Protocol targeting the Cloudflare Worker's /mcp endpoint: [Box / VPS Node] │ ├─► Test 1: git_read (key: "wrong") ──────────────────┐ │ ▼ │ [Worker /mcp Endpoint] │ │ │ HTTP 401 Unauthorized │ │ ◄───────────────────────────────────────────────────┘ │ Payload: {"error": "bad or missing key"} [PASS] │ ├─► Test 2: git_read (key: $RUNNER_KEY, ──────────────┐ │ path: OPERATIONS/VAULT/...) ▼ │ [Worker /mcp Endpoint] │ │ │ HTTP 200 OK (Authorization Refusal) │ │ ◄───────────────────────────────────────────────────┘ │ Payload: "vault refused" [PASS] │ └─► Test 3: git_read (key: $RUNNER_KEY, ──────────────┐ path: PHONE-DROPS/...) ▼ [Worker /mcp Endpoint] │ HTTP 200 OK │ ◄───────────────────────────┘ Payload: JSON/Markdown Content [PASS] 1. Test 1 (Wrong Key Failure): The Box issues a git_read call passing an intentionally invalid key ("key": "wrong"). The worker returns an HTTP 401 Unauthorized status with payload "bad or missing key", confirming authentication enforcement. 2. Test 2 (Vault Path Refusal): The Box issues a git_read call using its valid $RUNNER_KEY, targeting restricted vault paths (e.g., OPERATIONS/VAULT/22-OPERATIONAL-KEYS.md). The worker refuses authorization, returning "vault refused", confirming path-based least-privilege resource scoping. 3. Test 3 (Allowed Path Read): The Box issues a git_read call using $RUNNER_KEY targeting allowlisted inbox paths under OPERATIONS/COMMANDSTATION/PHONE-DROPS/. The worker returns the file content, confirming valid read channels are operational. Analytical Evaluation ("So What?") Combining non-blocking kernel locks (flock -n) with the 3-step self-test protocol provides defense-in-depth and blast radius containment. If an execution process hangs during complex inference, kernel locks prevent cron schedules from spawning duplicate processes that exhaust system RAM. Furthermore, the 3-step self-test mathematically proves that even if an execution node were fully compromised, an attacker possessing the local $RUNNER_KEY could neither access sensitive vault directories nor extract administrative write tokens (GH_TOKEN), restricting potential exposure to isolated inbox drop files. Mathematical & Evidential Distinction: REAL vs. HOLLOW * REAL Rating: Earned exclusively when a stage's claims are verified by physical, disk-backed files, explicit SHA-256 digests, and line checks. * HOLLOW Rating: Assigned when evidence is incomplete, unverified, or missing. * Fail-Closed Sealing Law: Any HOLLOW rating in the audit chain blocks session sealing at Stage 7 (DEATH), programmatically halting downstream campaign execution. Analytical Evaluation ("So What?") Replacing traditional soft logging with the fail-closed REAL versus HOLLOW gate model alters system verification. In conventional agent frameworks, an agent can report completion based solely on unverified LLM output ("hallucinated success"). In PaiS, session closure (DEATH) is programmatically impossible unless every lifecycle transition produces verifiable disk receipts, byte-level character counts, and cryptographic digests. This guarantees audit compliance across continuous agent handoffs. Stage 9 & Stage 10: Keyless Git Shuttle (phone_drop) & System Synthesis Context & Strategic Importance Connecting control planes, serverless layers, and persistent muscle nodes without exposing write credentials requires dedicated messaging primitives. Stage 9 and Stage 10 define the keyless shuttle lane (phone_drop) and synthesize the complete 10-stage architecture into a cohesive, self-healing "Life Loop." Stage 9 — Keyless Git Shuttle Mechanics (phone_drop) The phone_drop MCP tool exposed by the Cloudflare Worker (pais-mcp-cloud) serves as the keyless write transport lane uniting distributed system nodes: * HTTP Interface: Exposed over streamable POST /mcp tool calls. * Tag Sanitization Syntax: Input tags undergo regular expression sanitization to enforce lowercasing, strip special characters, and eliminate directory traversal attempts (../): tag = String(tag || "").toLowerCase().replace(/[^a-z0-9-]/g, "-").replace(/-+/g, "-").slice(0, 40) || "untagged"; * Destination Restriction: Output file writes are strictly restricted to the allowlisted directory OPERATIONS/COMMANDSTATION/PHONE-DROPS/ on the GitHub master branch. * Payload Ceilings: Single drops are capped at 50 KB per drop with a single-file transaction ceiling. * Multi-File Concatenation: Multi-file updates are concatenated into a single drop separated by explicit file headers: [FILE: filename.md] <file content> Upon ingestion, the receiving daemon parses header delimiters and splits the payload into individual files on disk.
